Theorem 216.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: For convergence analysis pairs (A,B) in model theory, the coupling constant satisfies g ≤ 846 and the invariant I(A,B) = 591 mod 2.

Proof: We apply the convergence analysis principle to the model decomposition. By minimality, the element a^846 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 591-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.

Theorem 216.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Under the structural decomposition assumption on model structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 331. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 292 with relations in degree 2.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 331 is immediate from the structural decomposition property. For the inductive step, we decompose the model structure using the structural decomposition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 292. Each subproblem has a solution by the structural decomposition fixed-point theorem with constant 2. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
